\displaystyle\frac{2(-1)^{J+1}\left(\Delta_{\psi}+J-1\right)\Gamma\left(J+\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{1}{2}\right){}^{2}\Gamma\left(J+2\Delta_{\psi}-1\right)}{\Gamma(J)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right){}^{2}\Gamma\left(2J+2\Delta_{\psi}-1\right)}\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
0&1+(-1)^{J}\end{pmatrix},

(4.4.19)

\displaystyle P^{(s)}_{[\psi_{\alpha}\psi^{\alpha}]_{n,J}}=

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{J+1}2^{-2J}J\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}\right)\Gamma\left(J+\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(J+2\Delta_{\psi}\right)}{\left(1-2\Delta_{\psi}\right){}^{2}\Gamma(J+1)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(2\Delta_{\psi}-2\right)\Gamma\left(J+\Delta_{\psi}\right)}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1+(-1)^{J}}{J\left(J+2\Delta_{\psi}-2\right)}&0\\
0&\frac{1-(-1)^{J}}{(J+1)\left(J+2\Delta_{\psi}-1\right)}\\
\end{array}\right).

After an appropriate change of basis, these results match perfectly to those calculated using the lightcone bootstrap at large
J

[
1
]
.

4.4.4
MFT Coefficients for Higher Twist Towers

In the subsections above we presented the MFT coefficients for the leading twist towers; here we will present the results for higher twist towers.

For
\expectationvalue{\f\psi\psi\f}:

\displaystyle P_{11}^{(s)}\left([\phi\psi]_{n,J}\right)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{(2J+1)\Gamma(J+1)\cos\left(\pi\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}\right)\right)\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{\psi}-1\right)\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{\phi}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(J+n+\Delta_{\psi}\right)}{2^{4-2\Delta_{\phi}}\pi^{3/2}n!\Gamma\left(J+\frac{3}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}-1\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(2\Delta_{\phi}-1\right)\Gamma(J+n+1)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4J+4n-3\right)\Gamma\left(J+n+\Delta_{\phi}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(-2n-\Delta_{\phi}-\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{7}{2}\right)}{\Gamma\left(2J+2n+\Delta_{\phi}+\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{1}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{\phi}+\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{5}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(J+n+\Delta_{\phi}+\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(J+2n+\Delta_{\phi}+\Delta_{\psi}-1\right)}{\Gamma\left(J+2n+\Delta_{\phi}+\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{3}{2}\right)},

(4.4.20a)

\displaystyle P_{22}^{(s)}\left([\phi\partial\psi]_{n,J}\right)=

\displaystyle\,P_{11}^{(s)}\left([\phi\psi]_{n,J}\right)\frac{\left(\Delta_{\psi}+n-1\right)\left(\Delta_{\phi}+J+n-\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}+n-\frac{5}{2}\right)}{2(J+n+1)\left(\frac{1}{4}\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}-5\right)+n\right)\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}+J+2n-\frac{3}{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\times\frac{\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}+J-1\right)+n\right)}{\left(J+\frac{1}{4}\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4n-3\right)\right)}\;.

(4.4.20b)

For
\expectationvalue{\psi_1\psi_2\psi_2\psi_1}:

P^{(s)}_{[\psi_{1\alpha}\psi_{2\beta}]_{n,J}}=-\begin{pmatrix}\frac{n\left(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+2J+2n-2\right)}{4J}c_{1}&c_{2}\\
c_{2}&\left(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+J+2n-2\right)\left(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+2J+2n-2\right)c_{3}\end{pmatrix}\\
\times\frac{\Gamma\left(J+n+\Delta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(J+n+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}-\frac{5}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(J+2n+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}-3\right)}{\Gamma\left(2n+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}-3\right)\Gamma\left(J+2n+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}-\frac{5}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(2J+2n+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}-1\right)}\\
\times\frac{\Gamma\left(J+\frac{3}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{1}-1\right)\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{2}-1\right)\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}-3\right)\Gamma\left(J+n+\Delta_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{(J+1)n!\Gamma\left(\Delta_{1}-1\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{2}-1\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(J)\Gamma\left(J+n+\frac{3}{2}\right)},

(4.4.21)

and

P^{(s)}_{[\psi_{1\alpha}\psi_{2}^{\alpha}]_{n,J}}=-2^{2\Delta_{1}+2\Delta_{2}-6}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1+J}{2}d_{1}&d_{2}\\
-d_{2}&-\frac{J}{2}d_{3}\end{pmatrix}\\
\times\frac{\Gamma\left(J+\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+2J+2n-1\right)\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{1}-1\right)\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{2}-1\right)\Gamma\left(n+\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}-2\right)}{\pi\left(2\Delta_{1}-1\right)\left(2\Delta_{2}-1\right)(J+1)n!\Gamma\left(2\Delta_{1}-2\right)\Gamma\left(2\Delta_{2}-2\right)\Gamma(J+1)\Gamma\left(J+n+\frac{3}{2}\right)}\\

4.5
CFT data of a general spectrum

In this section, we will use the techniques we developed in previous section to calculate the anomalous dimensions of the double-twist operators exchanged in the
\expectationvalue{\psi\f\f\psi}
and
\expectationvalue{\psi\psi\psi\psi}
correlators. For this, we will primarily focus on using the coefficient of the double poles in eqn. (
A.2
) to obtain
\delta hP
, and we will divide it by
P^{\text{MFT}}
to obtain
\delta h=\gamma/2
.
24
24

24

One can further improve these results by considering the residues of eqn. (
A.2
) to obtain
\delta P
as in (
A.2.9
) or the example in
4.5.2
, with which one schematically has
\delta h=\frac{\delta hP}{P^{\text{MFT}}+\delta P}
up to possible mixing between different twist towers. Necessary
\mathcal{K}
coefficients needed for these computations are provided in
[
2
]
.
We also give an example of calculating OPE coefficient corrections in section
4.5.2
.

4.5.1
Applications in the computation of anomalous dimensions

As we reviewed in section
4.3.5
,
6j
symbols develop double poles when certain relations are satisfied; in the case of eqn. (
A.2
), we have double poles in
\Delta^{\prime}
at
\Delta^{\prime}=\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+J^{\prime}
if
\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}=\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{4}
. Here these correspond to double-twist operators
[\phi_{1}\phi_{2}]
. We will stick to decomposition coefficients
\mathcal{K}
with
\mathcal{O}_{6}^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}_{6}
, so taking the coefficient of double poles of eqn. (
A.2
) roughly translates into relating
\delta hP_{[\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}]}\evaluated{}_{\cO_6}
to
\delta hP_{[\phi_{1}\phi_{2}]}\evaluated{}_{\cO_6}
, where here
\phi_{i}
are some fictitious scalar operators. For later convenience, we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J,n}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\mathcal{O}_{6})\equiv

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+J+2n}\left(\Delta-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}-J-2n\right)^{2}\frac{S(\phi_{3}\phi_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5}])}{\eta^{(s)}_{\mathcal{O}_{5}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,J}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{dp}_{2}^{J,n}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\mathcal{O}_{6})\equiv

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+J+2n}\left(\Delta-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}-J-2n\right)^{2}\frac{S(\phi_{3}\phi_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5}])}{\eta^{(s)}_{\mathcal{O}_{5}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,J}\end{array}\right),

which describe the corrections
\delta hP
with external scalars after the OPE coefficients are stripped off:

\displaystyle\delta hP_{[\phi_{1}\phi_{2}]}\evaluated{}_{\cO_6\in\phi_1\x\phi_1}=

\displaystyle\lambda_{\phi_{1}\phi_{1}\mathcal{O}_{6}}\lambda_{\phi_{2}\phi_{2}\mathcal{O}_{6}}\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J,n}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\mathcal{O}_{6})\;,

(4.5.2)

\displaystyle\delta hP_{[\phi_{1}\phi_{2}]}\evaluated{}_{\cO_6\in\phi_1\x\phi_2}=

\displaystyle\lambda_{\phi_{1}\phi_{2}\mathcal{O}_{6}}^{2}\mathfrak{dp}_{2}^{J,n}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\mathcal{O}_{6})\;.

As a reminder, in these formulas
\eta
is the normalization for the scalar partial wave.

The particular decomposition we will be using in eqn. (
A.2
) will be the one with
\mathcal{O}_{6}
kept constant, hence by taking the coefficients of double poles on both sides, we obtain

(\delta hP)_{ab}([\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}]_{n,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t),fg}_{\cO_6}}=\\
-\lambda_{326,f}\lambda_{146,g}\sum\limits_{\phi_{i},J^{\prime},n^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}^{fg}_{ab}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&[\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}]_{n,J}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}&[\phi_{1}\phi_{2}]_{n^{\prime},J^{\prime}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J^{\prime},n^{\prime}}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\mathcal{O}_{6})\\
-\lambda_{326,f}\lambda_{146,g}\sum\limits_{\phi_{i},J^{\prime},n^{\prime}}\mathcal{K}^{fg}_{ab}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&[\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{2}]_{n,J}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&[\phi_{1}\phi_{2}]_{n^{\prime},J^{\prime}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\mathfrak{dp}_{2}^{J^{\prime},n^{\prime}}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\mathcal{O}_{6})\;.

(4.5.3)

For example, for the leading parity-even tower in the s-channel of
\expectationvalue{\psi\f\f\psi}
, we find

(\delta hP)_{11}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\cO_6}}=-i\lambda_{\phi\phi\mathcal{O}_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\mathcal{O}_{6}}^{1}\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J-\frac{1}{2},0}(\psi^{\frac{1}{2}},\phi,\mathcal{O}_{6})\\
-i\lambda_{\phi\phi\mathcal{O}_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\mathcal{O}_{6}}^{2}(1-\delta_{0,\ell_{6}})\bigg(\frac{\left(J+\frac{1}{2}\right)(J+1)}{\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\ell_{6}}\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J+\frac{1}{2},0}(\psi^{-\frac{1}{2}},\phi,\mathcal{O}_{6})\\
+\frac{2\left(\Delta_{\psi}+J-1\right){}^{2}\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}+J-2\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+2J-5\right)}{\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\ell_{6}\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4J-5\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4J-3\right)}\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J-\frac{1}{2},0}(\psi^{-\frac{1}{2}},\phi,\mathcal{O}_{6})\\

For the parity-odd tower, we instead have

(\delta hP)_{22}\left([\phi\psi]^{-}_{0,J}\right)\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\cO_6}}=i\lambda_{\phi\phi\mathcal{O}_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\mathcal{O}_{6}}^{1}\bigg(\frac{J+\frac{1}{2}}{J+1}\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J+\frac{1}{2},0}(\psi^{\frac{1}{2}},\phi,\mathcal{O}_{6})\\

+i\lambda_{\phi\phi\mathcal{O}_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\mathcal{O}_{6}}^{2}(1-\delta_{0,\ell_{6}})\bigg(\frac{1}{\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\ell_{6}}\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J-\frac{1}{2},1}(\psi^{-\frac{1}{2}},\phi,\mathcal{O}_{6})\\

+\frac{\left(2\Delta_{\phi}+2J-1\right){}^{2}\left(-2\Delta_{\psi}-\Delta_{6}+\ell_{6}+4\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}-\Delta_{6}+\ell_{6}-1\right)\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}+J-1\right)}{2\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\ell_{6}\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+2J-3\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4J-3\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4J-1\right)}\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J-\frac{1}{2},0}(\psi^{\frac{1}{2}},\phi,\mathcal{O}_{6})\bigg)\;.

(4.5.5)

We would like to point out the appearance of
\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J-\frac{1}{2},1}(\psi^{-\frac{1}{2}},\phi,\mathcal{O}_{6})
which indicates that we may need to extract the data of
non-leading twist
scalar towers from the scalar
6j
symbol in order to obtain the
leading twist
spinning towers. This happens for the cases where the scaling dimensions of
\mathcal{O}_{1,2}
and
\ell_{5}
are shifted downwards while the scaling dimension of
\mathcal{O}_{5}
is shifted upwards. We also note that the absence of
\lambda_{\psi\psi\mathcal{O}_{6}}^{3,4}
follows from our parity-definite choice of three-point structures.

26

26

The OPE coefficients

\expectationvalue{\f\f\cO_6}\expectationvalue{\psi\psi\cO_6}^{3,4}

in embedding space (see footnote
5

\expectationvalue{\psi\f\cO_5}^{1,2}\expectationvalue{\psi\f\cO_5}^{2,1}
for which no double pole appears:
(\delta hP)_{12}\left([\phi\psi]_{0,J}\right)=(\delta hP)_{21}\left([\phi\psi]_{0,J}\right)=0
.

(4.5.7)

\displaystyle+\frac{\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+J-2\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+J-1\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2J-3\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2J-1\right)\left(4\Delta_{\psi}+2J-5\right)}{64\left(\Delta_{\psi}+J-1\right){}^{2}\left(4\Delta_{\psi}+2J-3\right)}\mathfrak{dp}_{2}^{J-1,0}(\psi^{-\frac{1}{2}},\psi^{\frac{1}{2}},\phi_{6})

\displaystyle+\frac{J\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+J-1\right)\left(8\Delta_{\psi}^{3}+2\left(4J^{2}-8J+3\right)\Delta_{\psi}-4J^{2}+4(4J-3)\Delta_{\psi}^{2}+4J-3\right)}{8\left(2\Delta_{\psi}-1\right)\left(\Delta_{\psi}+J-1\right)\left(\Delta_{\psi}+J\right)}\mathfrak{dp}_{2}^{J,0}(\psi^{-\frac{1}{2}},\psi^{\frac{1}{2}},\phi_{6})\bigg),

To calculate the scalar coefficients

\displaystyle\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J,0}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\mathcal{O}_{6})

\displaystyle=-\sum\limits_{p=0}^{\infty}\sum\limits_{q=\text{max}(-p,p-2J_{6})}^{p}2\kappa^{0,0}_{2\bar{h}}\sin(\pi(h_6-2h_1))\sin(\pi(h_6-2h_2))

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\times\mathcal{A}^{0,0}_{p,q}\Omega^{h_{1}h_{2}h_{2}h_{1}}_{\bar{h},h_{6}+p,2h_{2}}\frac{\Gamma(2(\bar{h}+q))}{\Gamma^{2}(\bar{h}+q)}\bigg|_{\bar{h}=h_{1}+h_{2}+J},

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{A}^{h_{21},h_{12}}_{p,q}\Omega^{h_{1}h_{2}h_{1}h_{2}}_{\bar{h},h_{6}+p,h_{1}+h_{2}}\frac{\Gamma(2(\bar{h}+q))}{\Gamma(h_{12}+\bar{h}+q)\Gamma(h_{21}+\bar{h}+q)}\bigg|_{\bar{h}=h_{1}+h_{2}+J}.

(4.5.10)

Here we have defined

,

\displaystyle\kappa^{a,b}_{2\bar{h}}=\frac{\Gamma(\bar{h}+a)\Gamma(\bar{h}-a)\Gamma(\bar{h}+b)\Gamma(\bar{h}-b)}{2\pi^{2}\Gamma(2\bar{h})\Gamma(2\bar{h}-1)}\,.

(4.5.11)

The coefficients
\mathcal{A}^{a,b}_{p,q}
come from performing dimensional reduction for 3d blocks in terms of the chiral, 2d blocks and were found for

in
[

]
.
27
27

27

In comparison to
[
215
]
we use
\mathcal{A}^{\text{here}}_{p,q}=\mathcal{A}^{\text{there}}_{\frac{p_{q}}{2},\bar{h}-h+q-p}
, and for Eq. (2.35) there we use
c^{(d)}_{\ell}=\frac{(d-2)_{\ell}}{\left(\frac{d-2}{2}\right)_{\ell}}
.
For general
a
and

we can compute
\mathcal{A}^{a,b}_{h,\bar{h}}
recursively using the Casimir equation. For explicit results, we will mainly be interested in the large spin asymptotics, in which case we can restrict to
p=q=0
and use
\mathcal{A}^{a,b}_{0,0}=1
. Finally, the function
\Omega
is given by

:

\displaystyle\Omega^{h_{1},h_{2},h_{3},h_{4}}_{h_{5},h_{6},p}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma(2h_{5})\Gamma(h_{6}-p+1)\Gamma(h_{5}+h_{12}-h_{6}+p-1)\Gamma(-h_{12}+h_{34}+h_{6}-p+1)}{\Gamma(h_{5}+h_{12})\Gamma(h_{5}+h_{34})\Gamma(h_{5}-h_{12}+h_{6}-p+1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{}_{4}F_{3}\biggl[\genfrac{.}{.}{0.0pt}{}{h_{23}+h_6,h_6-h_{14},-h_{12}+h_{34}+h_6-p+1,h_6-p+1}{2 h_6,h_5-h_{12}+h_6-p+1,-h_5-h_{12}+h_6-p+2};1\biggr]

\displaystyle+\frac{\Gamma(2h_{6})\Gamma(h_{5}+h_{13}+p-1)\Gamma(h_{5}+h_{42}+p-1)\Gamma(-h_{5}-h_{12}+h_{6}-p+1)}{\Gamma(h_{6}-h_{14})\Gamma(h_{23}+h_{6})\Gamma(h_{5}+h_{12}+h_{6}+p-1)}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt{}_{4}F_{3}\biggl[\genfrac{.}{.}{0.0pt}{}{h_5+h_{13}+p-1,h_5+h_{42}+p-1,h_5+h_{34},h_5+h_{12}}{h_5+h_{12}+h_6+p-1,2 h_5,h_5+h_{12}-h_6+p};1\biggr]\,.

(4.5.12)

When we study double-twist operators with large spin, or equivalently large
\bar{h}
, the first
{}_{4}F_{3}
hypergeometric yields the asymptotic, large-spin prediction while the second
{}_{4}F_{3}
gives effects which are exponentially suppressed.

By inserting eqn. (
4.5.1
) and eqn. (
4.5.1
) into eqn. (
4.5.3
), we can obtain
\delta hP
for various double-twist operators of fermions. Below we will consider some examples.

\delta hP
of double-twist towers
[\phi\psi_{\alpha}]^{\pm}_{0,\ell_{5}}
due to scalar exchange:

From eqn. (
4.5.4
) and eqn. (
4.5.5

(\delta hP)_{11}^{(p)}=-\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}\frac{(-1)^{\ell_{5}+1}\Gamma\left(\Delta_{6}\right)\left(\sin\left(\pi\left(-\Delta_{\psi}-\Delta_{\phi}+\Delta_{6}\right)\right)+\sin\left(\pi\left(\Delta_{\psi}-\Delta_{\phi}\right)\right)\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)}\\
\times\Omega^{\frac{2\Delta_{\psi}+1}{4},\frac{\Delta_{\phi}}{2},\frac{\Delta_{\phi}}{2},\frac{2\Delta_{\psi}+1}{4}}_{\frac{2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4\ell_{5}-1}{4},\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2},\Delta_{\phi}}

(4.5.13a)

and

(\delta hP)_{22}^{(p)}=-\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}\frac{(-1)^{\ell_{5}}\Gamma\left(\Delta_{6}\right)\cos\left(\pi\left(\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)\right)\sin\left(\pi\left(\Delta_{\phi}-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)\right)}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)^{2}}\\
\times\Bigg(\frac{\left(2\ell_{5}+1\right)}{\ell_{5}+1}\Omega^{\frac{2\Delta_{\psi}+1}{4},\frac{\Delta_{\phi}}{2},\frac{\Delta_{\phi}}{2},\frac{2\Delta_{\psi}+1}{4}}_{\frac{2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4\ell_{5}+3}{4},\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2},\Delta_{\phi}}\\

(4.5.13b)

By expanding the perturbative terms at large
\ell

\displaystyle(\delta hP)_{11}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,\ell_{5}})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\f_6}}\sim{}

\displaystyle{}\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}(-1)^{\ell_{5}+1}\Gamma\left(\Delta_{6}\right)2^{-\Delta_{\psi}-\Delta_{\phi}-2\ell_{5}+\frac{5}{2}}\ell_{5}^{\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}-\Delta_{6}-1}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right){}^{2}\Gamma\left(-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}+\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\phi}-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)},

(4.5.14a)

\displaystyle(\delta hP)_{22}([\phi\psi]^{-}_{0,\ell_{5}})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\f_6}}\sim{}

\displaystyle{}\frac{\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}(-1)^{\ell_{5}}\left(-2\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{6}+2\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta_{6}+1\right)\right)\ell_{5}^{\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}-\Delta_{6}-2}}{2^{\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}-\Delta_{6}+2\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}}\Gamma\left(\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}+\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\phi}-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)}.

(4.5.14b)

By dividing these by the MFT coefficients at large

\displaystyle\gamma_{[\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,\ell_{5}}}\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\f_6}}={}

\displaystyle{}\frac{2}{P_{11}^{(s)}}(\delta hP)_{11}\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\f_6}}=\frac{i\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}}{\ell_{5}^{\Delta_{6}}}\frac{2^{\Delta_{6}}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta_{6}+1\right)\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\phi}\right)}{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma\left(\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(-\Delta_{6}+2\Delta_{\psi}+1\right)\right)\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(2\Delta_{\phi}-\Delta_{6}\right)\right)},

(4.5.15a)

\displaystyle\gamma_{[\phi\psi]^{-}_{0,\ell_{5}}}\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\f_6}}={}

(4.5.15b)

\delta hP
of parity-even double-twist tower
[\phi\psi_{\alpha}]^{+}_{0,\ell_{5}}
due to stress tensor exchange:

By inserting eqn. (

\times\Bigg(\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+1\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4\ell_{5}-3\right){}_{4}F_{3}\biggl[\genfrac{.}{.}{0.0pt}{}{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2},\frac{3}{2}-\Delta_{\phi},1-\Delta_{\psi}}{1,l_5+1,-l_5-\Delta_{\phi}-\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{5}{2}};1\biggr]\\

+\text{ (non-perturbative terms)},

where

\displaystyle(\delta hP)_{11}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,\ell_{5}})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{T}}\sim\frac{3i(-1)^{\ell_{5}}\Delta_{\phi}2^{-\Delta_{\psi}-\Delta_{\phi}-2\ell_{5}+4}\ell_{5}^{\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}-2}}{\pi^{3/2}c_{T}\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}-1\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\phi}-\frac{1}{2}\right)},

(4.5.18)

and we obtain the anomalous dimension via eqn. (

(4.5.19)

4.5.2
Computation of corrections to OPE Coefficients: a working example

In eqn. (
A.2
) we relate the OPE function of spinning operators to the scalar
6j
symbols, which we reproduce for reader’s convenience:

\rho_{ac}^{(s)}(\mathcal{O})S^{c}_{b}(\mathcal{O}_{3}\mathcal{O}_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}])\evaluated{}_{G^{(t),fg}_{\cO_6}}=\lambda_{326,f}\lambda_{146,g}\sum\limits_{\phi_{i},\mathcal{O}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}}\mathcal{K}^{fg}_{ab}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\\
\times\qquad\frac{S(\phi_{3}\phi_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}])}{\eta^{(s)}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\\
\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\end{array}\right).

(4.5.26)

By taking the double poles in
\Delta
on both sides, we can extract
\delta hP
for spinning operators in terms of scalar data, which we detailed and illustrated in section
4.5.1
. In this section, we will use this equation to extract correction to OPE coefficients for double twist operators
[\phi\psi]^{+}_{0}
due to an exchange of a scalar in the crossed channel.

We see in eqn. (
4.5.4
) that the
\delta hP
for
[\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J}
reads as

\displaystyle(\delta hP)_{11}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=

\displaystyle-i\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}\mathfrak{dp}_{1}^{J-\frac{1}{2},0}(\psi^{\frac{1}{2}},\phi,\phi_{6})\;,

(4.5.27)

\displaystyle(\delta hP)_{12}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=

\displaystyle(\delta hP)_{21}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=(\delta hP)_{22}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=0\;,

as only the first term in eqn. (
A.2.11
) contributes. For
(\delta P)
on the other hand, we do not need double poles (single poles are sufficient) and there are also cross terms, hence we have

\displaystyle(\delta P)_{11}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=

\displaystyle-i\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}\mathfrak{p}_{1,+}^{J-\frac{1}{2},0}\;,

(4.5.28)

\displaystyle(\delta P)_{12}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=

\displaystyle-\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{3}\bigg(\frac{i\left(2\ell_{5}+1\right)}{\Delta_{6}-1}\mathfrak{p}_{1,-}^{J+\frac{1}{2},0}

\displaystyle+\frac{8i\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\ell_{5}-1\right)\left(\Delta_{\phi}+\ell_{5}-1\right)\left(\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}+\ell_{5}-2\right)}{\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4\ell_{5}-5\right)\left(2\Delta_{\psi}+2\Delta_{\phi}+4\ell_{5}-3\right)}\mathfrak{p}_{1,-}^{J-\frac{1}{2},0}\bigg)\;,

\displaystyle(\delta P)_{12}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=

\displaystyle i\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{3}\frac{1}{\Delta_{6}-1}\mathfrak{p}_{2,-}^{J-\frac{1}{2},0}\;,

\displaystyle(\delta P)_{22}([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,J})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=

\displaystyle 0\;,

where we define the shorthand notation

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{i,\pm}^{J,n}\equiv\mathfrak{p}_{i}^{J,n}(\psi^{\pm},\phi,\phi,\psi^{\frac{1}{2}},\phi_{6})

(4.5.29)

for

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{1}^{J,n}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3},\phi_{4},\mathcal{O}_{6})\equiv

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+J+2n}\left(\Delta-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}-J-2n\right)\frac{S(\phi_{3}\phi_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\Delta,J}])}{\eta^{(s)}_{\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,J}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,J}\end{array}\right),

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{2}^{J,n}(\phi_{1},\phi_{2},\phi_{3},\phi_{4},\mathcal{O}_{6})\equiv

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\Delta\rightarrow\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{4}+J+2n}\left(\Delta-\Delta_{3}-\Delta_{4}-J-2n\right)\frac{S(\phi_{3}\phi_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{\Delta,J}])}{\eta^{(s)}_{\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,J}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,J}\end{array}\right).

We can compute
\mathfrak{p}
similar to
\mathfrak{dp}
and include both perturbative and nonperturbative corrections to OPE coefficients. For brevity, we only reproduce the leading piece of the perturbative correction at large
\ell
:

(\delta P)([\phi\psi]^{+}_{0,\ell_{5}})\evaluated{}_{G^{(t)}_{\phi_6}}=\frac{i\sqrt{\pi}(-1)^{\ell_{5}-\frac{1}{2}}2^{-\Delta_{\psi}-\Delta_{\phi}+\Delta_{6}-2\ell_{5}+\frac{5}{2}}\ell_{5}^{\Delta_{\psi}+\Delta_{\phi}-\Delta_{6}-1}}{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}+\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\phi}-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)}\\
\times\begin{pmatrix}-\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}\frac{\Gamma\left(\frac{\Delta_{6}+1}{2}\right)H_{\frac{\Delta_{6}-2}{2}}}{\sqrt{\pi}\Gamma\left(\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)}&\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{3}\frac{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}+\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\sqrt{\ell_{5}}\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)}\\
\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{3}\frac{\Gamma\left(-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}+\Delta_{\psi}+\frac{1}{2}\right)}{2\sqrt{\ell_{5}}\Gamma\left(\Delta_{\psi}-\frac{\Delta_{6}}{2}\right)}&0\end{pmatrix},

(4.5.31)

where
H_{a}
is the Harmonic number. As a consistency check, we see that setting

\displaystyle\Delta_{6}\rightarrow 0\;,\quad\lambda_{\phi\phi\phi_{6}}\rightarrow 1\;,\quad\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{1}\rightarrow i\;,\quad\lambda_{\psi\psi\phi_{6}}^{3}\rightarrow 0

(4.5.32)

reduces the result to the MFT coefficient eqn. (
4.4.11a
).

Chapter 5
Discussion

In this thesis we reviewed various analytic tools that have been developed in recent years and used them to extend the progress of the analytic conformal bootstrap program to fermionic conformal field theories in three dimensional spacetimes.

In the first part of the thesis, we used the
\mathrm{SL}(2,\mathbb{R})
expansion of the conformal blocks and the known formulae for the summation of the
Casimir-irregular
terms to derive the relation between the CFT data of different channels in the lightcone bootstrap. This old-style lightcone bootstrap has been used for scalars and is sufficient to derive the large spin behavior of the double twist families in the spectrum, although the results surprisingly match the numerics even at low spins for scalar theories such as the
3d
Ising and
\mathrm{O}(N)
models. We extended this technique to fermions and derived the OPE coefficients and the anomalous dimensions for the large spin spectrum of fermionic CFTs. In particular, we provided both leading and next-to-leading order terms in the large spin expansion.

In the second part of the thesis, we provided a different approach for the analytic analysis of CFTs with spinning operators (such as fermions). This approach relies on deriving relations between the
6j
symbols of the conformal group and using the known
6j
symbols for scalars. The relations between the
6j
symbols are derived using the conformally invariant pairings of the three point structures and the differential operators that can be used to shift the conformal weights of a given operator. When combined, these technologies allow one to compute the CFT data of fermionic operators
non-perturbatively
, that is by including both the perturbative and exponentially suppressed terms in spin. The second piece is inaccessible with the traditional lightcone bootstrap that we used in the first part of the thesis, and it ensures that the result is analytic all the way down to low spins, yielding expressions far more compatible with the numerical results. We first demonstrated the importance of these non-perturbative pieces in the
3d
Ising and
\mathrm{O}(N)
models, then extended the calculations to generic unitary fermionic CFTs by deriving the analytic expressions for their double twist spectrum.

While extending the application of the Lorentzian inversion formula to spinning correlators, we derived relations between the
6j
symbols of different representations of the conformal group.
1
1

1

The reader can refer toeqn. (
A.1.25
) for this relation and the definition of
\mathcal{J}-
coefficients.
Aside from their employment in the construction of the OPE function (hence the derivation of the CFT data), the
\mathcal{J}-
coefficients that we have constructed for this relation may be useful on their own. As
6j
symbols are kinematic objects of the conformal group without any dynamic data, the relations between these objects are valid in any system with the conformal symmetry, and we believe that our results for these coefficients may have pure mathematical value, considering our derivation (and the resultant
J-
coefficients) are completely exact without any sort of approximation. We hope that further mathematical insight can be obtained from these objects in the future.

There are several open questions left that can be considered by the tools and results of this thesis. For instance, our demonstration for the improvement of the analytic predictions for
3d
Ising and
\mathrm{O}(N)
models with the inclusion of non-perturbative effects can be extended to concrete fermionic CFTs, such as GNY models. Would we then expect new precise analytic predictions that match the results of the numerical bootstrap
[
81
,
226
]
? It is also natural to ask if we can predict analytic trajectories that cannot be accessed using scalar correlators in
3d

\mathcal{N}=1
SCFTs such as the supersymmetric Ising
[
233
,
234
]
and Wess-Zumino
[
235
]
models. This is an interesting question as the implications of imposing analytic bootstrap constraints for all external operators in the same supermultiplet has not yet been fully understood, so our results for external fermionic operators can be utilized to address this question in SCFTs.

Moreover, it is also straightforward to go to higher spin now that we understand how to spin down a fermionic
6j
symbol in
3d
. As a simple example, our results could then also be used to study correlation functions of conserved currents
J_{\mu}
in the
O(N)
vector model. There are many physically relevant observables, such
\expectationvalue{JJT}
, which are only accessible with spinning correlators. Based on
[
126
,
127
,
1
]
we now know that the current and stress tensor lie on the double-twist trajectories composed of the fundamental scalars,
\phi
, so these correlators are now within reach of analytic methods.

Finally, we note that our results are directly applicable to the study of Witten diagrams
with external fermionic operators. For example, by studying the contribution of the stress tensor
T^{\mu\nu}
to a fermionic correlator, e.g.
\expectationvalue{\phi\psi\psi\phi}
, we can derive the binding energy for a two-particle state dual to
[\phi\psi]_{n}
, due to tree-level graviton exchange. The anomalous dimension, or corresponding 6j symbol, can then be used to bootstrap a graviton loop in
AdS
4

[
236
,
133
]
. Likewise, by extending our computations to external currents, we can derive similar results for gauge interactions in AdS
4
, which would provide valuable data that can be cross-checked with perturbative bulk computations.
2
2

2

As part of his PhD work not included in this thesis
[
237
,
238
,
239
,
240
,
241
,
242
,
243
]
, the author studied AdS
4
gluons in momentum space, showing that written in a particular gauge any tree-level gluon Witten diagram can be purely computed algebraically (without any bulk-integration) which is demonstrated by the computation of the explicit results for several higher point amplitudes
[
237
,
238
]
. It would be interesting to connect to these bulk computations from a boundary perspective.
Lastly, if one wants to study an AdS theory with fermions, we need to understand the tree-level fermionic correlators to fully determine a one-loop scalar four point function. We therefore hope the results presented here are useful in the wider study of AdS
4
correlators.

Appendix A
6j
Symbols

In section
4.3.5
we defined and reviewed
6j
symbols. In this appendix, we will further discuss
6j
symbols and detail our method of deriving relations between
6j
symbols for operators of different spins.

A.1
Spinning Down the
6j
Symbol

We first draw the attention of the reader to the problem of inverting a single partial wave (or block) with external spinning operators. This allows us to compute corrections to the anomalous dimensions of double twist operators. As a reminder, the general form of the
6j
symbol is given by

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{array}\right\}^{abcd}

\displaystyle=\left(\widetilde{\Psi}^{(s),ab}_{\mathcal{O}_{5}},\Psi^{(t),cd}_{\mathcal{O}_{6}}\right)

\displaystyle=\int d^{d}x_{1}...d^{d}x_{6}\expectationvalue{\widetilde{\cO}_1\widetilde{\cO}_2\widetilde{\cO}_{5}}\expectationvalue{\widetilde{\cO}_3\widetilde{\cO}_4\cO_5}\expectationvalue{\cO_3\cO_2\cO_6}\expectationvalue{\cO_1\cO_4\widetilde{\cO}_{6}}.

(A.1.3)

Our strategy will in a way be the reverse of the scalar case
[
133
]
. Instead of using the Lorentzian inversion formula, we will follow the strategy outlined in
[
152
]
and use weight-shifting operators to calculate the
6j
symbol for external fermions in terms of the
6j
symbol for external scalars. We then use the expression (
4.3.5
), which splits the scalar
6j
symbol into two pieces from inverting the physical block and its shadow to find the corresponding split for the fermionic
6j
symbol.

To start, we use the results of
[
152
]
to write the t-channel spinning partial wave as a differential operator acting on the partial wave for external scalars:

\displaystyle\Psi^{(t),ab}_{\mathcal{O}}(x_{i})=\mathfrak{D}^{ab}_{t}\Psi^{(t),\text{scalar}}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}(x_{i})\;,

(A.1.4)

where
\Psi^{(t),\text{scalar}}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}(x_{i})
is a partial wave for four external scalars,
\expectationvalue{\f_3\f_2\f_1\f_4}
. We are being very schematic here and it should be understood that
\mathfrak{D}^{ab}_{t}
is a sum of multiple weight-shifting operators. For each term in the sum we may have to choose a different shifted operator
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}
, with scaling dimension and spin shifted from
\mathcal{O}
, as well as different external scaling dimensions
\Delta_{i}
of the scalar partial wave.

Given this expression we can simplify the spinning
6j
symbol by taking the adjoint of this operator:

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{\Psi}^{(s),ab}_{\mathcal{O}_{5}},\Psi^{(t),ab}_{\mathcal{O}_{6}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\mathfrak{D}^{ab}_{s}\widetilde{\Psi}^{(s),\text{scalar}}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}},\mathfrak{D}^{ab}_{t}\Psi^{(t),\text{scalar}}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\widetilde{\Psi}^{(s),\text{scalar}}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}},\mathfrak{D}^{*,ab}_{s}\mathfrak{D}^{ab}_{t}\Psi^{(t),\text{scalar}}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}}\right).

(A.1.5)

The two weight-shifting operators acting on the t-channel conformal partial wave then give a linear combination of undifferentiated t-channel conformal partial waves, at the price of more shifts for the internal and external labels. In the end we are left with an equality of the form

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{array}\right\}^{abcd}=\sum\limits_{\phi_{i},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5,6}}\mathcal{J}^{abcd}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\\
\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}\end{array}\right\},

where the sum runs over some set of scaling dimensions for the fictitious external scalars
\phi_{i}
, whose dimensions are related to
\mathcal{O}_{i}
by some (half-)integer shift, and over both scaling dimensions and spins for
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5,6}
, which are again related to the
\mathcal{O}_{5,6}
by (half-)integer shifts in both labels. We now turn to how to compute these coefficients.

The general strategy to compute the decomposition factors
J^{abcd}
in eqn. (
A.1
) can be systematized by the following procedure:

•

Write a three-point structure in terms of weight-shifting operators acting on three-point structures of lower spins.

•

Use integration by parts and crossing symmetry of covariant three-point structures to move the weight-shifting operators such that they act on the same operator.

•

By using irreducibility of the representations, the weight-shifting operators become multiples of the identity.

•

Repeat until all three-point structures are of the form
\expectationvalue{\f\f\cO}
, i.e. we are left with three-point functions involving two scalars.

Below, we will unpack this procedure further by detailing each step in the explicit decomposition of the
6j
symbol of two external scalars and two external fermions.

In our conventions, the
6j
-symbol for the correlator
\expectationvalue{\phi\phi\psi\psi}
reads as

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{matrix}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{matrix}\right\}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}=\int d^{d}x_{1}...d^{d}x_{6}\expectationvalue{\tl\f_1\tl\f_2\tl\cO_5}\expectationvalue{ \tl\psi_3\tl\psi_4\cO_5}^{s_{2}}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\phi_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\expectationvalue{ \f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}},

(A.1.12)

where the first three-point structure is already in the appropriate form for a
6j
symbol of four external scalars, so all we need to do is to massage the remaining structures. As the first step, we use eqn. (
4.3.104
) to rewrite
\expectationvalue{\tl\psi_3\tl\psi_4\cO_5}^{s_{2}}
:
1
1

1

We use the same shorthand notation as is used in section
4.5.1
:
\phi^{a}_{i}\equiv\phi_{\Delta_{i}+a}
,
\widetilde{\phi}^{a}_{i}\equiv\phi_{3-\Delta_{i}+a}
,
\mathcal{O}_{i}^{a,b}\equiv\mathcal{O}_{\Delta_{i}+a,\ell_{i}+b}
, and
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{i}^{a,b}\equiv\mathcal{O}_{3-\Delta_{i}+a,\ell_{i}+b}
.

\int d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\expectationvalue{\tl\psi_3\tl\psi_4\cO_5}^{s_{2}}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\expectationvalue{ \f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}}\\
=\int d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\sum\limits_{a,b}\kappa^{s_{2}}_{3,ab}(\widetilde{\psi}_{3}\widetilde{\psi}_{4}\mathcal{O}_{5})\mathcal{D}_{1}^{-a,+}\mathcal{D}_{2}^{-b,+}\expectationvalue{\tl\f_3^a\tl\f_4^b\cO_5 }\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\expectationvalue{\f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}}\;.

(A.1.13)

After that, we integrate by parts to obtain

\int d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\expectationvalue{\tl\psi_3\tl\psi_4\cO_5}^{s_{2}}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\expectationvalue{ \f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}}=\int d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\\
\sum\limits_{a,b}\kappa^{s_{2}}_{3,ab}(\widetilde{\psi}_{3}\widetilde{\psi}_{4}\mathcal{O}_{5})\expectationvalue{\tl\f_3^a\tl\f_4^b\cO_5 }\left[\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}^{-a,+}\right)^{*}_{A}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\right]\left[\left(\mathcal{D}_{2}^{-b,+}\right)^{A*}\expectationvalue{\f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}}\right]\;,

(A.1.14)

where we are showing the spinor indices of weight-shifting operators explicitly. Witheqn. (
4.3.94
), the equation further reduces to

\displaystyle\int d^{d}x_{3}

\displaystyle d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\expectationvalue{\tl\psi_3\tl\psi_4\cO_5}^{s_{2}}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\expectationvalue{ \f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\sum\limits_{a,b}\kappa^{s_{2}}_{3,ab}(\widetilde{\psi}_{3}\widetilde{\psi}_{4}\mathcal{O}_{5})\zeta_{0}^{-a,+}\zeta_{0}^{-b,+}\expectationvalue{\tl\f_3^a\tl\f_4^b\cO_5 }

\displaystyle\times\left[\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}^{-a,-}\right)_{A}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\right]\left[E_{\phi\psi\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\phi\psi}^{t_{2}t_{2}^{\prime}}\left(\mathcal{D}_{3}^{-b,-}\right)^{A}\expectationvalue{\tl\cO_6\f_1\psi_4}^{t_{2}^{\prime}}\right],

(A.1.15)

where we defined the exchange matrix
E

\displaystyle\expectationvalue{\cO_2\cO_1\cO}^{a}=E^{ab}_{21\mathcal{O}\to 12\mathcal{O}}\expectationvalue{\cO_1\cO_2\cO}^{b}

(A.1.16)

to reorder the last tensor structure so that the weight-shifting operator acts on the third operator.
2
2

2

We do this because we will use the convention of
[
152
]
for finite-dimensional representations, where the weight-shifting operator acts on the first (third) operator in the
s
(
t
) channel.
We can then use the crossing for conformally covariant three-point structures as derived in
[
152
]
, which reads as

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{D}^{-a,-b}_{3}\right)^{A}\expectationvalue{\cO_1\cO_2\cO_3^{a,b}}^{m}=\sum_{c,d,n}\left\{\begin{matrix}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{1}^{c,d}\\
\mathcal{O}_{3}&S&\mathcal{O}_{3}^{a,b}\end{matrix}\right\}^{mn}\left(\mathcal{D}^{-c,-d}_{1}\right)^{A}\expectationvalue{\cO_1^{c,d}\cO_2\cO_3}^{n}

(A.1.17)

for the fermionic representation of weight-shifting operators in
3d
. Thus we obtain

\displaystyle\int d^{d}x_{3}

\displaystyle d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\expectationvalue{\tl\psi_3\tl\psi_4\cO_5}^{s_{2}}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\expectationvalue{ \f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\sum\limits_{a,b}\kappa^{s_{2}}_{3,ab}(\widetilde{\psi}_{3}\widetilde{\psi}_{4}\mathcal{O}_{5})\zeta_{0}^{-a,+}\zeta_{0}^{-b,+}\expectationvalue{\tl\f_3^a\tl\f_4^b\cO_5 }

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\left[\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}^{-a,-}\right)_{A}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\right]\left[E_{\phi\psi\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\phi\psi}^{t_{2}t_{2}^{\prime}}\sum_{c,d}\left\{\begin{matrix}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}&\phi_{1}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}^{c,d}\\
\phi_{4}^{-b}&S&\psi_{4}\end{matrix}\right\}^{t_{2}^{\prime}\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}}\left(\mathcal{D}^{-c,-d}_{1}\right)^{A}\expectationvalue{\tl\cO_6\f_1\phi_4^{-b}}\right].

(A.1.18)

We can integrate by parts and use the crossing again to get both weight-shifting operators to act on the same operator:

\displaystyle\int

\displaystyle d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\expectationvalue{\tl\psi_3\tl\psi_4\cO_5}^{s_{2}}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\expectationvalue{ \f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\sum\limits_{a,b,c,d}\kappa^{s_{2}}_{3,ab}(\widetilde{\psi}_{3}\widetilde{\psi}_{4}\mathcal{O}_{5})\zeta_{0}^{-a,+}\zeta_{0}^{-b,+}\zeta_{\ell_{t}+d}^{-c,-d}\left\{\begin{matrix}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}&\phi_{1}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}^{c,d}\\
\phi_{4}^{-b}&S&\psi_{4}\end{matrix}\right\}^{t_{2}^{\prime}\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}}E_{\phi\psi\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\phi\psi}^{t_{2}t_{2}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\times\expectationvalue{\tl\f_3^a\tl\f_4^b\cO_5 }\Bigg[\sum_{e,f,n}\left\{\begin{matrix}\psi_{3}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}^{e,f}\\
\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}&S&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{matrix}\right\}^{t_{1}n}\left(\mathcal{D}_{1}^{-a,-}\right)_{A}\left(\mathcal{D}^{-e,-f}_{1}\right)^{A}\expectationvalue{\cO_3^{e,f}\f_2\cO_6^{-c,d}}^{n}\Bigg]\expectationvalue{\tl\cO_6\f_1\phi_4^{-b}}.

(A.1.19)

By the irreducibility of the representations, we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{D}^{-a,-b}\right)_{A}\left(\mathcal{D}^{c,d}\right)^{A}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,\ell}=\delta^{ac}\delta^{bd}\beta^{\Delta,\ell}_{ab}\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,\ell}

(A.1.20)

with which we finally obtain
3
3

3

One can derive
\beta
by acting on the two point function with weight shifting operators in embedding space. In our conventions, we have

\displaystyle\beta_{a,b}^{\Delta,\ell}\equiv b(a+2\Delta-3)(b+2\ell+1)(2ab\Delta+a(2b+1)(a+b-2)+2\ell)\quad\text{ for }a,b=\pm 1\cdot

(A.1.21)

\displaystyle\int

\displaystyle d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\expectationvalue{\tl\psi_3\tl\psi_4\cO_5}^{s_{2}}\expectationvalue{\psi_3\f_2\cO_6}^{t_{1}}\expectationvalue{ \f_1\psi_4\tl\cO_6}^{t_{2}}

\displaystyle=d^{d}x_{3}d^{d}x_{4}d^{d}x_{6}\sum\limits_{a,b,c,d}\beta_{a,+}^{\Delta_{3}-a,0}\kappa^{s_{2}}_{3,ab}(\widetilde{\psi}_{3}\widetilde{\psi}_{4}\mathcal{O}_{5})\zeta_{0}^{-a,+}\zeta_{0}^{-b,+}\zeta_{\ell_{t}+d}^{-c,-d}E_{\phi\psi\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\phi\psi}^{t_{2}t_{2}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\begin{matrix}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}&\phi_{1}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}^{c,d}\\
\phi_{4}^{-b}&S&\psi_{4}\end{matrix}\right\}^{t_{2}^{\prime}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}}\left\{\begin{matrix}\psi_{3}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}^{-a}\\
\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}&S&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{matrix}\right\}^{t_{1}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}}\expectationvalue{\tl\f_3^a\tl\f_4^b\cO_5 }\expectationvalue{\phi_3^{-a}\f_2\cO_6^{-c,d}}^{n}\expectationvalue{\f_1\phi_4^{-b}\tl\cO_6^{c,d}}.

(A.1.22)

Inserting this into eqn. (
A.1.12
), we get

\left\{\begin{matrix}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{matrix}\right\}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}=\sum\limits_{a,b,c,d}\mathcal{J}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}^{-a}&\phi_{4}^{-b}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\\
\times\left\{\begin{matrix}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}\\
\phi_{3}^{-a}&\phi_{4}^{-b}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{matrix}\right\}\;,

(A.1.23)

where

\mathcal{J}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}^{-a}&\phi_{4}^{-b}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}\end{array}\right)\normalsize=\beta_{a,+}^{\Delta_{3}-a,0}\kappa^{s_{2}}_{3,ab}(\widetilde{\psi}_{3}\widetilde{\psi}_{4}\mathcal{O}_{5})\zeta_{0}^{-a,+}\zeta_{0}^{-b,+}\zeta_{\ell_{t}+d}^{-c,-d}\\
\times E_{\phi\psi\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\phi\psi}^{t_{2}t_{2}^{\prime}}\left\{\begin{matrix}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}&\phi_{1}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}^{c,d}\\
\phi_{4}^{-b}&S&\psi_{4}\end{matrix}\right\}^{t_{2}^{\prime}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}}\left\{\begin{matrix}\psi_{3}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}^{-a}\\
\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}&S&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{matrix}\right\}^{t_{1}\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}}.

(A.1.24)

Note that if
\ell_{5}=0
, there are only 2 independent structures for
\expectationvalue{\psi_3\psi_4\phi_5}^{s_{2}}
, hence we need to take a
2\times 2
invertible submatrix of
\kappa_{3}
. We can do this by restricting to the structures
\expectationvalue{\psi_3\psi_4\phi_5}^{1,3}
in eqn. (
3.1.15
), and fixing
b=\frac{1}{2}
in eqn. (
A.1.23
) instead of summing over
b=\pm\frac{1}{2}
.

Despite the complicated and lengthy expressions, the procedure is actually quite straightforward and most easily tractable in the diagrammatic notation, see figure
A.1
for a summary of the decomposition above. However, one should only use the diagrammatic expressions as a guide, as there are ambiguities in their meaning, most notably sign ambiguities as they do not carry the information of the order of operators in the equations.

\Rightarrow

\begin{aligned} \includegraphics[scale]{ssff3}\end{aligned}

\Rightarrow

\begin{aligned} \includegraphics[scale]{ssff4}\end{aligned}

\Rightarrow

\begin{aligned} \includegraphics[scale]{ssff5}\end{aligned}

\Rightarrow

\begin{aligned} \includegraphics[scale]{ssff6}\end{aligned}

Figure A.1:

Step by step diagrammatic illustration for the decomposition of the
6j
symbol for
\expectationvalue{\phi\psi\psi\phi}
into the
6j
symbol for
\expectationvalue{\f\f\f\f}
. The idea is as follows: one re-expresses a fermionic three-point structure,
\expectationvalue{\psi\psi\cO}
, in terms of weight-shifting operators acting on a scalar three-point structure,
\expectationvalue{\phi\phi\cO}
. The weight-shifting operators are then moved inside the diagrammatic loop until they act on the same leg. By the irreducibility of the representations, i.e.
\left(\mathcal{D}^{a,b}\right)_{A}\left(\mathcal{D}^{-c,-d}\right)^{A}\sim\delta^{ac}\delta^{bd}
, the diagram reduces to that of a scalar
6j
symbol. To be able to move around the weight-shifting operators, one either integrates by parts or uses the crossing relation for covariant three-point structures as explained in the main text. The diagrams above correspond to the equations eqn. (
A.1.12
), eqn. (
A.1.13
), eqn. (
A.1
), eqn. (
A.1
), eqn. (
A.1.19
), and eqn. (
A.1.22
) respectively.

For spinning correlators beyond
\expectationvalue{\f\f\psi\psi}
we can repeat the procedure above and recursively spin-down the
6j
symbol. For this, we first define a generalized form of eqn. (
A.1
):

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{array}\right\}^{abcd}=\sum\limits_{\phi_{i},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5,6}}\mathcal{J}^{abcd}_{efgh}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{2}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{3}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{1}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{2}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\\
\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{3}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}\end{array}\right\}^{efgh},

(A.1.25)

where we obtain the ultimate result
J^{abcd}
of eqn. (
A.1
) by summing over these intermediate factors
J^{abcd}_{efgh}
.

For four external fermions, we only need to repeat this process twice, where in the first step we reduce from four external fermions to two external fermions and two external scalars, and then in the second step we reduce from two external fermions and two external scalars to four external scalars. The second step is already what we derived above, so the only new ingredient is the first step:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\psi_{1}&\psi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{array}\right\}^{s_{1}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}=\sum\limits_{a,b,c,d,t_{1}^{\prime},t_{2}^{\prime}}\mathcal{J}^{s_{1}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}_{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}^{\prime}t_{2}^{\prime}}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\psi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\\
\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}\\
\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{array}\right\}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}^{\prime}t_{2}^{\prime}}.

(A.1.26)

Combining this with eqn. (
A.1.23
), we obtain the final result

\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\psi_{1}&\psi_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{array}\right\}^{s_{1}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}=\sum\limits_{a,\dots,h}\mathcal{J}^{s_{1}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\psi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\phi_{3}^{-e}&\phi_{4}^{-f}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c-g,d+h}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\\
\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c-g,d+h}\\
\phi_{3}^{-e}&\phi_{4}^{-f}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{array}\right\},

(A.1.27)

where

\mathcal{J}^{s_{1}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\psi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\phi_{3}^{-e}&\phi_{4}^{-f}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c-g,d+h}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\\
=\sum\limits_{t_{1}^{\prime},t_{2}^{\prime}}\mathcal{J}^{s_{1}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}_{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}^{\prime}t_{2}^{\prime}}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\psi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\\
\times\mathcal{J}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}^{\prime}t_{2}^{\prime}}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}\\
\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\phi_{3}^{-e}&\phi_{4}^{-f}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c-g,d+h}\end{array}\right)\normalsize.

(A.1.28)

We can derive
J^{s_{1}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}_{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}^{\prime}t_{2}^{\prime}}
in a similar manner to how we derived
J^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}
. For brevity we skip the intermediate steps, illustrated diagrammatically in figure
A.2
, and only present the final result here:

\mathcal{J}^{s_{1}s_{2}t_{1}t_{2}}_{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}s_{2}t_{1}^{\prime}t_{2}^{\prime}}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\psi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-a}&\phi_{2}^{-b}&\psi_{3}&\psi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}\end{array}\right)\normalsize=\sum\limits_{u_{1},u_{2},u_{1}^{\prime},u_{2}^{\prime}}\kappa^{s_{1}}_{3,ab}(\widetilde{\psi}_{1}\widetilde{\psi}_{2}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{5})\zeta_{0}^{-b,+}\zeta_{0}^{-a,+}\zeta_{\ell_{t}+d}^{-c,-d}\\
\times\beta_{b,+}^{\Delta_{2}-b,0}E_{\psi_{1}\psi_{2}\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\psi_{2}\psi_{1}\mathcal{O}}^{t_{1}u_{1}}\left\{\begin{matrix}\psi_{2}&\psi_{3}&\phi_{2}^{-b}\\
\mathcal{O}_{6}^{-c,d}&S&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{matrix}\right\}^{u_{1}u_{1}^{\prime}}E_{\phi\psi\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\psi\phi\mathcal{O}}^{u_{1}^{\prime}t_{1}^{\prime}}E_{\psi_{1}\psi_{2}\mathcal{O}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}\psi_{2}\psi_{1}}^{t_{2}u_{2}}\\
\times\left\{\begin{matrix}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}&\psi_{4}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{6}^{c,d}\\
\phi_{1}^{-a}&S&\psi_{1}\end{matrix}\right\}^{u_{2}u_{2}^{\prime}}E_{\mathcal{O}\psi\phi\rightarrow\phi\psi\mathcal{O}}^{u_{2}^{\prime}t_{2}^{\prime}}\;.

(A.1.29)

Step by step diagrammatic illustration for the decomposition of
6j

A.2
OPE Function and its Decomposition

In section
4.3.5
we discussed how
6j
symbols are related to OPE coefficients. We reproduce eqn. (
4.3.89
) for convenience:

\displaystyle\lambda_{125,a}\lambda_{345,b}\bigg|_{G^{(t),fg}_{\mathcal{O}_{6}}}

\displaystyle=-\Res_{\Delta=\Delta_{5}}\rho_{ac}^{(s)}(\mathcal{O})S^{c}_{b}(\mathcal{O}_{3}\mathcal{O}_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}])\bigg|_{G^{(t),fg}_{\mathcal{O}_{6}}}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{1+\Sigma_{55}+\Sigma_{12}+\Sigma_{13}+\Sigma_{23}}\lambda_{326,f}\lambda_{146,g}

\displaystyle\quad\times\Res_{\Delta=\Delta_{5}}\eta_{(ad)(ce)}^{\Delta,J}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,J}\end{array}\right)^{defg}S^{c}_{b}(\mathcal{O}_{3}\mathcal{O}_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}])\bigg|_{J=J_{5}}.

We aim to relate the inversion of a single block for external fermions to the inversion of a single block for a scalar four-point function. For that, by comparing (
4.3.5
), (
4.3.5
), and (
A.1
) we write down

\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\end{array}\right)^{abcd}=(-1)^{\Sigma_{66}}\left(S^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}_{6}}])\right)^{d}_{e}\\
\times\sum\limits_{\phi_{i},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5,6}}\mathcal{J}^{abce}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize K^{\phi_{1}\phi_{4}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{6}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\\
\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}\end{array}\right).

(A.2.3)

We then find the following expression for the OPE function:

\displaystyle\rho_{ac}^{(s)}(\mathcal{O})S^{c}_{b}(\mathcal{O}_{3}\mathcal{O}_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}])\evaluated{}_{G^{(t),fg}_{\cO_6}}=\lambda_{326,f}\lambda_{146,g}\sum\limits_{\phi_{i},\mathcal{O}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}}

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{fg}_{ab}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{S(\phi_{3}\phi_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}])}{\eta^{(s)}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\\
\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}\end{array}\right),

where
\eta^{(s)}_{\mathcal{O}_{5}}
is the normalization of the scalar partial wave for
\expectationvalue{\f_1\f_2\f_3\f_4}
and we have defined

\mathcal{K}^{fg}_{ab}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\equiv(-1)^{\Sigma_{55}+\Sigma_{66}+\Sigma_{12}+\Sigma_{13}+\Sigma_{23}}K^{\phi_{1}\phi_{4}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}_{6}}\frac{S^{c}_{b}(\mathcal{O}_{3}\mathcal{O}_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}])}{S(\phi_{3}\phi_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}^{\prime}])}\\
\times\left(S^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{O}_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}_{6}}])\right)^{g}_{h}\eta_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}^{(s)}\eta_{(ad)(ce)}^{(s)\mathcal{O}}\mathcal{J}^{defh}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize.

(A.2.8)

We expect the physical poles for inverting a fermionic block to come from the physical poles from inverting a scalar block. We then have for example

\lambda_{125,a}\lambda_{345,b}\evaluated{}_{G^{(t),fg}_{\cO_6}}=-\lambda_{326,f}\lambda_{146,g}\sum\limits_{\phi_{i},\mathcal{O}^{\prime},\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}}\mathcal{K}^{fg}_{ab}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{5}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize\\
\times\Res_{\Delta=\Delta_{5}}\frac{S(\phi_{3}\phi_{4}[\widetilde{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{\Delta,J}}])}{\eta^{(s)}_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{\Delta,J}}}\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\phi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\mathcal{O}^{\prime}_{6}\\
\phi_{3}&\phi_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{\Delta,J}^{\prime}\end{array}\right)\evaluated{}_{J=J_5}.

(A.2.9)

Here we have assumed the inversion of a single block just has single poles. In general when studying a correlator
\expectationvalue{\cO_1\cO_2\cO_3\cO_4}
with
\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}=\Delta_{3}+\Delta_{4}
we find both single and double poles. The double poles give the OPE coefficients times the anomalous dimensions while the single poles gives the OPE coefficients themselves
[
127
]
.

Equation
A.2
is the main result of this approach: by using weight-shifting operators successively, we can express CFT data of spinning operators in terms of
6j
symbols of external scalars and the decomposition coefficients
\mathcal{K}
. The former can be calculated efficiently using the Lorentzian inversion formula whereas the latter is given in terms of partial wave normalization factors, shadow matrices, and
6j
symbol decomposition coefficients
\mathcal{J}
, each of which we have computed explicitly.

We presented the most general form in eqn. (
A.2
), however one can in fact choose either
\mathcal{O}_{5}
or
\mathcal{O}_{6}
to stay the same by moving the weight-shifting operators through the other leg only. Indeed, we kept
\mathcal{O}_{5}
the same in the calculation of the
\mathcal{J}
coefficients both for
\expectationvalue{\f\f\psi\psi}
and for
\expectationvalue{\psi\psi\psi\psi}
, as we can observe in eqn. (
A.1.23
) and eqn. (
A.1.27
). One can similarly compute
\mathcal{J}
while keeping
\mathcal{O}_{6}
constant, though a separate calculation is not necessary: there are several identities between various
\mathcal{J}
coefficients, which follow from the symmetries of the
6j
symbols that we have summarized in appendix
A.4
. In particular, via eqn. (
A.4.5
), we have

\mathcal{J}^{abcd}_{efgh}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\mathcal{O}_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{3}&\mathcal{O}_{4}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\mathcal{O}_{1}^{\delta_{1},\epsilon_{1}}&\mathcal{O}_{2}^{\delta_{2},\epsilon_{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{3}^{\delta_{3},\epsilon_{3}}&\mathcal{O}_{4}^{\delta_{4},\epsilon_{4}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\delta_{5},\epsilon_{5}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{\delta_{6},\epsilon_{6}}\end{array}\right)\normalsize=(-1)^{\Sigma_{55}+\Sigma_{5^{\prime}5^{\prime}}+\Sigma_{66}+\Sigma_{6^{\prime}6^{\prime}}}\\
\times\mathcal{J}^{dcba}_{hgfe}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{1}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{4}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{3}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{2}&\mathcal{O}_{6}&\mathcal{O}_{5}\\
\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{1}^{-\delta_{1},\epsilon_{1}}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{4}^{-\delta_{4},\epsilon_{4}}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{3}^{-\delta_{3},\epsilon_{3}}&\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}_{2}^{-\delta_{2},\epsilon_{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}^{\delta_{6},\epsilon_{6}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\delta_{5},\epsilon_{5}}\end{array}\right)\normalsize.

(A.2.10)

Let us now turn to the explicit results for the
\mathcal{K}^{fg}_{ab}
coefficients. Despite the complicated intermediate steps, the final form they take is quite simple as they are relatively short meromorphic functions in scaling dimensions and spins. For example, the only nonzero
\mathcal{K}
coefficients for
\expectationvalue{\psi\f\f\psi}
arising from the exchange of a scalar
\phi_{6}
in the t-channel are

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{11}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\phi_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize={}

\displaystyle{}i,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{11}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\phi_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize={}

\displaystyle{}\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}-\Delta_{251}+\frac{5}{2}\right)^{2}}{4\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\ell_{5}+1\right)\left(\ell_{5}-\Delta_{5}+2\right)\left(\ell_{5}-\Delta_{5}+3\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{22}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\phi_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize={}

\displaystyle{}-\frac{i\left(\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}\right)}{\ell_{5}+1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{22}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\phi_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize={}

\displaystyle{}-\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\Delta_{251}+\ell_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)^{2}}{4\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-1\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}3}_{12}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\phi_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize={}

\displaystyle{}\frac{i\left(\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}-\Delta_{125}+\frac{5}{2}\right)}{\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\left(\ell_{5}+1\right)},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}3}_{12}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\phi_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize={}

\displaystyle{}\frac{i\left(\Delta_{152}+\ell_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\Delta_{251}+\ell_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)}{4\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)^{-1}\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-2\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\times\frac{\left(\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}+\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-\frac{9}{2}\right)}{\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-1}\;,

(A.2.11m)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}3}_{21}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\phi_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize={}

\displaystyle{}\frac{i\left(\Delta_{125}+\ell_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)}{\Delta_{6}-1},

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.89871pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}3}_{21}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\phi_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize={}

\displaystyle{}\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}-\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}-\Delta_{5}+\frac{11}{2}\right)}{4\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\left(\ell_{5}+1\right)\left(\ell_{5}-\Delta_{5}+2\right)}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\times\frac{\left(\ell_{5}-\Delta_{152}+\frac{5}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}-\Delta_{251}+\frac{5}{2}\right)}{-\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}+3},

(A.2.11r)

We would like to emphasize two points. Firstly, as there is not a unique way to write eqn. (
A.2.9
), the statement that the
\mathcal{K}
in eqn. (
A.2.11
) are the only nonzero coefficients for
\expectationvalue{\psi\f\f\psi}
with an exchange of a scalar
\phi_{6}
in the t-channel should be understood for a particularly chosen decomposition in eqn. (
A.2.9
). One can of course change the decomposition, which would then require a new set of
\mathcal{K}
coefficients. For example, we used a set of
\mathcal{K}
coefficients with
\mathcal{O}_{6}
held constant in eqn. (
A.2.11
); another set with
\mathcal{O}_{5}
held constant instead can be immediately obtained via eqn. (
A.2.10
).
4
4

4

It should be noted that not all different decompositions are related to each other via symmetries. For example,
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{\prime}=\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}
whereas
\mathcal{O}_{1}^{\prime}=\phi_{1}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
in eqn. (
A.2.11
): this follows from fixing
b=\frac{1}{2}
in eqn. (
A.1.24
) as we noted after the equation. If we were to fix
b=-\frac{1}{2}
instead, we would then have a set of
\mathcal{K}
coefficients with
\mathcal{O}_{4}^{\prime}=\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
and
\mathcal{O}_{1}^{\prime}=\phi_{1}^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}
, and these new coefficients are not related to eqn. (
A.2.11
) in any manifestly symmetric way.
Secondly, we note that the absence of
\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{12}
,
\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{21}
,
\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}3}_{11}
, and
\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-2.1097pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}3}_{22}
is not coincidental: they are forbidden by the parity symmetry as we work in a parity-definite basis.
5
5

5

We would like to caution the reader that this statement follows from the symmetries of three-point structures
\expectationvalue{\cO_1\cO_2\cO_3}^{a}
under the transformation
X\rightarrow-X
in embedding space, hence it is true whether the relevant physical theory has parity symmetry or not, i.e.
\expectationvalue{\cO_1\cO_2\cO_3}^{a}
are merely formal entities and should not be thought of as physical three-point structures.

A.3
\mathcal{K}
Coefficients

In this appendix, we present the explicit expression for the
\mathcal{K}
coefficients defined in eqn. (
A.2.8
) for
\expectationvalue{\psi\f\f\psi}
. As there are a different number of three-point tensor structures depending on whether
\ell_{5,6}=0
, the minimal complete set of nonzero
\mathcal{K}
coefficients differs for each case. We already presented the results for
\expectationvalue{\psi\f\f\psi}
with
\ell_{6}=0
in eqn. (
A.2.11
), so we will detail the
\expectationvalue{\psi\f\f\psi}
with
\ell_{6}\neq 0
below. For
\expectationvalue{\psi_1\psi_2\psi_2\psi_1}
and
\expectationvalue{\psi\psi\psi\psi}
, the coefficients become quite lengthy so we do not reproduce them here; please see the
Mathematica
file of
[
2
]
for their explicit expressions.

For the correlator
\expectationvalue{\psi\phi\phi\psi}
, the list below constitutes a sufficient set of nonzero
\mathcal{K}
coefficients if the exchanged operator in t-channel is not a scalar.
6
6

6

For scalar exchange in the t-channel, see eqn. (
A.2.11
).
For convenience, we will use a different shorthand notation in this section, i.e.

\displaystyle\Delta_{abc}=\Delta_{a}+\Delta_{b}+\Delta_{c}\;,\quad\Delta_{ab}^{c}=\Delta_{a}+\Delta_{b}-\Delta_{c}.

(A.3.1)

The coefficients are:

\begin{array}[]{cc}\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}2}_{11}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize$
}&\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\Delta_{125}+\ell_{5}-\frac{9}{2}\right){}^{2}\left(\Delta_{15}^{2}+\ell_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right){}^{2}}{16\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\ell_{6}\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-1\right)}\\
\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}2}_{22}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize$
}&-\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(-\Delta_{125}+\ell_{5}+\frac{11}{2}\right){}^{2}\left(-\Delta_{15}^{2}+\ell_{5}+\frac{5}{2}\right){}^{2}}{16\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\left(\ell_{5}+1\right)\ell_{6}\left(-\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}+2\right)\left(-\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}+3\right)}\\
\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}2}_{22}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize$
}&-\frac{i\left(\Delta_{12}^{5}+\ell_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right){}^{2}}{4\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\ell_{6}}\\
\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}2}_{11}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize$
}&\frac{i\left(\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(-\Delta_{12}^{5}+\ell_{5}+\frac{5}{2}\right){}^{2}}{4\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\left(\ell_{5}+1\right)\ell_{6}}\\
\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}3}_{12}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{-\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize$

\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{22}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\

}&-\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\Delta_{25}^{1}+\ell_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right){}^{2}}{4\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-1\right)}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{cc}\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}2}_{22}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\

}&\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\Delta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(-\Delta_{6}+\ell_{6}-1\right)\right)\left(\Delta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta_{6}-\ell_{6}-4\right)\right)\left(\Delta_{25}^{1}+\ell_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right){}^{2}}{4\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\ell_{6}\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}-1\right)}\\

}&\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(-\Delta_{25}^{1}+\ell_{5}+\frac{5}{2}\right){}^{2}}{4\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\ell_{5}+1\right)\left(-\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}+2\right)\left(-\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}+3\right)}\\
\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}2}_{11}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\

}&-\frac{i\left(\Delta_{5}-\frac{3}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{5}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left(\Delta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(-\Delta_{6}+\ell_{6}-1\right)\right)\left(\Delta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta_{6}-\ell_{6}-4\right)\right)\left(-\Delta_{25}^{1}+\ell_{5}+\frac{5}{2}\right){}^{2}}{4\left(\Delta_{5}-2\right)\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\left(\ell_{5}+1\right)\ell_{6}\left(-\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}+2\right)\left(-\Delta_{5}+\ell_{5}+3\right)}\\
\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{11}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{5}^{\frac{1}{2},-\frac{1}{2}}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\end{array}\right)\normalsize$
}&i\\
\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}2}_{11}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\

}&-\frac{i\left(\Delta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(-\Delta_{6}+\ell_{6}-1\right)\right)\left(\Delta_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta_{6}-\ell_{6}-4\right)\right)}{\left(\Delta_{6}-1\right)\ell_{6}}\\
\scalebox{.8}{
$\mathcal{K}^{\raisebox{-1.68776pt}{\scalebox{1.8}{$\cdot$}}1}_{22}\footnotesize\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}\psi_{1}&\phi_{2}&\phi_{2}&\psi_{1}&\mathcal{O}_{5}&\mathcal{O}_{6}\\
